After a 16-year long dev period, Faraway, a shooting star arcade game about siphoning the energy of other stars, is out now

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Lord almighty, video games take a while to make, 'ey? Especially if you're just one person working on it by yourself. Such is the case with Faraway, out today, an arcade game that reminds me of the early days of PS3 indies "about a shooting star that creates constellations to stay alive," all made single-handedly by creator of cult-classic mobile game Eliss, Steph Thirion. Did I mention that Faraway took 16 years to make? Like I said, game development is a lengthy process.

Looking at Faraway, and knowing how long it took to produce, it doesn't surprise me to come from a dev who managed to release a mobile game in the early days of the platform, long before free-to-play got its chokehold on the industry. It also really does feel like it should be sitting amongst the likes of PixelJunk, this thing should be something I'm desperate to play on my PS3 while I wait for my controller to charge.

As mentioned, you are a shooting star, a "spark meant to shine and vanish in a blink." But still you persist, connecting to and wrapping yourself around starts to "harvest their energy," in turn lengthening your own life, and of course, in arcadey fashion, racking up a nice score. It looks like game design as poetry, everything you're looking at procedurally generated to bring freshness to every run. I can fully envision this as a "one more run" kind of arcade game, the best kind, especially in an age long past the need to shove in another of your hard earned coins.

Faraway conceptually charms me because it taps into a style of game we don't get to see much of any more. What I'm saying is, I'm just a teensy bit sick of how many roguelikes there are, but that doesn't mean I don't want a game I can just hop in and out of at my own whims. Sure, arcade games might not be in vogue, I just miss them a bit. In any case, Faraway is available to pick up on Steam right now. Seems like it'll be worth a punt!

Newcastle star Amar Dedic sparks six-car police swoop after being spotted with a BOW AND ARROWS in Northumberland beauty spot before weapon is seized

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Published: 13:29 EDT, 5 November 2026 | Updated: 17:28 EDT, 5 November 2026

Newcastle United star Amar Dedic was at the centre of a major police operation after he was spotted carrying a bow and arrows at a Northumberland beauty spot – with at least SIX patrol cars involved in the search for the £30million defender.

The Bosnia international sparked alarm when a concerned member of the public reported seeing him and a friend walking with the weapon near a reservoir in the countryside outside Newcastle.

Police dispatched marked and unmarked vehicles to the area in Harlow Hill, with some understood to have been armed response units, before Dedic and his companion were finally intercepted around seven miles away in the exclusive Darras Hall estate.

Sources have described the dramatic swoop on Dedic's vehicle before officers searched the car and seized a bow and arrows, with the 24-year-old questioned about his activities.

In a bizarre twist, Newcastle goalkeeper Mark Gillespie and former defender Paul Dummett arrived at the scene while police were speaking to Dedic. It is understood the pair were in the area by chance and stopped to offer assistance.

After speaking to the two men, officers were satisfied there was no threat to the public, with police subsequently stating that the equipment was believed to have been intended for recreational use.

Amar Dedic played in Newcastle's 1-0 defeat by Everton on Monday evening

Dedic was spotted with a crossbow off Military Road, the 61-mile B6318 in Northumberland

However, enquiries remain ongoing into the incident, which happened on Wednesday afternoon. It is understood that Dedic and his friend had fired some arrows while in the countryside, although it remains unclear what they were aiming at. Hunting any animal or bird with a bow and arrow or crossbow is illegal in the UK.

Dedic joined Newcastle from Benfica during the summer and has impressed at right back despite the club's difficult start to the season.

Daily Mail Sport understands he will not face disciplinary action from Newcastle over the incident, although there is a feeling that the player was naive to believe he could walk around the countryside carrying such weaponry.

A Northumbria Police spokesperson said: 'Shortly after 1.55pm on Wednesday, we received a report of two men carrying a bow and arrows while walking along a track by the reservoir on Military Road in the Harlow Hill area.

'Officers attended and subsequently stopped a vehicle in the Darras Hall area and seized a bow and arrows. At this stage, it is understood they were for recreational use and there was no threat to the public. Enquiries are ongoing.'

Dedic is expected to be available for Saturday's Premier League trip to Fulham, where Newcastle will attempt to halt a run of four defeats in five matches across all competitions.

Swansea City receive significant boost as Vitor Matos issues injury update on key defender

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Swansea City have hit a poor run of form after a phenomenal start to the season. The Swans were top of the pile after 12 games, but a run of three defeats in four games has seen them drop to 4th, with a midweek defeat to Portsmouth the latest in that run.

During that Pompey clash, summer signing Stephen Welsh went off with a knee problem that appeared serious. However, as they head to Birmingham City this weekend, Vitor Matos has revealed that the injury is not as bad as first thought and he could be in contention to face Chris Davies’ side, as reported by Wales Online.

Matos said: “We will still assess if he will be available for Saturday but it’s not a bad injury. It’s just a knock that is really really painful.”

Welsh has made a great impression since making the switch from Celtic in the summer. Eom Jisung and Moussa Yeo are back available for the Swans this weekend as well, while Ben Cabango is also fit despite a recent knock.

Welsh has quickly established himself as one of Swansea City’s most important players since arriving on a free transfer from Celtic in summer 2026. The 26-year-old signed a two-year contract, with the option of a further year, becoming Vitor Matos’ first summer recruit.

Cameron Burgess’ long-term Achilles injury opened the door for Welsh, who has since made the centre-back position his own. He has started 13 of Swansea’s 14 Championship matches, primarily alongside Ben Cabango, and has impressed with his composure, defensive work and ability to play out from the back.

His passing accuracy, progressive distribution and contribution to Swansea’s defensive organisation have made him an ideal fit for Matos’ possession-based approach. His performances have also earned him his first senior Scotland caps.

With Swansea challenging near the top of the table, Welsh has become a key figure. Securing an experienced defender of his quality on a free transfer is proving to be excellent summer business.

Swansea are short of centre-back options without Welsh, although their defensive depth remains manageable in the short term.

Cabango is the obvious leader, while Jenson Seelt offers the most natural replacement after already featuring under Matos. The Sunderland loanee would be the likely partner for Cabango if Welsh is unavailable.

Filip Lissah provides further cover, having demonstrated his versatility by operating centrally, but regularly relying on him could weaken the right-back position. Cameron Burgess remains sidelined with a long-term Achilles injury, leaving Swansea more exposed if Welsh faces an extended absence.

Get root on a musl Linux system by slamming it with an overly long password

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Decade-old vulnerability in OpenSSH is now fixed, so get a-patchin'

On certain Linux systems, an attacker could gain root access by entering a password longer than 256 bytes. The vulnerability, dubbed *sshole by Wakefield researcher Özgün Kültekin, who discovered it, can only be exploited in very specific conditions.

The flaw affects certain Linux systems using musl libc instead of the traditional GNU C library, and even then only those running OpenSSH's secure shell daemon without Pluggable Authentication Modules (PAM) and with password authentication enabled.

If those conditions are met, and at least one other account has a valid password hash, accounts that shouldn't accept passwords, such as root, key-only deployment users, and LDAP users, may be accessed by supplying a password between 257 and 1,024 characters. The targeted accounts must have an asterisk (*) in their password field, and direct root login remains subject to OpenSSH's configuration.

That might seem niche, but sorry: It’s not.

Musl libc is common in embedded Linux systems due to its low footprint. More critically than that, it's also the standard C library for Alpine Linux, which provides official cloud images for both AWS and Azure. Some Alpine systems running in those environments may therefore be vulnerable, depending on their configuration.

The vulnerability in OpenSSH on musl-based Linux systems dates back to 2016, but Kültekin explains that its roots go back much further – to the BSD Unix era, actually.

In 1980, Sun Microsystems cofounder and BSD Unix heavyweight Bill Joy was involved in the development of BSD Unix, whose password file contained a single asterisk in the /etc/passwd entry for the daemon user. The asterisk wasn't a password hash and effectively disabled password authentication for that account.

Nowadays, password hashes typically live in /etc/shadow and contain hashed information that the system uses to verify a plaintext password entered by a user. Asterisks are still present for accounts that aren't supposed to accept password logins, but as Kültekin explains in his writeup, that isn't exactly a foolproof way to keep people out.

The crypt() function, which generates password hashes for comparison with stored values, can return NULL if it can't compute a hash. Applications that fail to check that result may crash. Musl libc's developers instead chose to return a single character for certain failures, and they picked the asterisk.

Musl also added SHA-512-based password hashing around the same time, which Kültekin notes gets slower the longer a password is, potentially leading to denial of service if someone floods a server with a super-long password. Musl capped passwords at 256 bytes, and you can likely see where this is going.

In 2016, OpenSSH introduced a workaround to prevent attackers from enumerating valid account names through SSH. Before the change, sshd could reject authentication attempts for nonexistent users more quickly than those for valid accounts. Attempts against valid accounts took longer because the server had to hash the supplied password before rejecting it. The solution was to have sshd perform dummy password hashing for invalid users, using an appropriate hashing algorithm and salt to reduce differences in response times.

On affected musl-based Linux systems, accounts marked with an asterisk instead of a password hash could be busted open by supplying a password longer than 256 bytes – a flaw dating back to 2016. Any musl-based Linux system running a vulnerable OpenSSH configuration without PAM, with password authentication enabled, at least one account marked with an asterisk, and another account with a valid password hash could be vulnerable to a *sshole attack.

Luckily, OpenSSH acted fast to fix this issue, and any OpenSSH build 10.6p2 (released Thursday) or newer is protected. Alternatively, if you can’t update your OpenSSH implementation on an affected system, just disable password authentication and restart the SSH daemon and you should be all set.

There’s no indication that vulnerable systems have been exploited, but if you want to check, Kültekin has included a simple CLI command in his writeup that can be used to check if any no-password accounts on a sensitive machine recently took a password.  ®

Hard West II for PS5, Xbox Series launches November 10

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Wild West-themed turn-based strategy game sequel Hard West II will launch for PlayStation 5 and Xbox Series on November 10, publisher Klabater and developer Ice Code Games announced.

Hard West II first launched for PC via Steam on August 4, 2022.

Here is an overview of the game, via Klabater:

Hard West II takes players deep into unforgiving wastelands, where revolver bullets collide with dark magic drawn straight from forgotten tales. Discover the story of Gin Carter, a legendary outlaw who sets out on a mission to rob the mysterious “Ghost Train.” However, it soon becomes clear that gold will not be your main objective—instead, you will face the task of reclaiming your own soul.

To achieve this, together with your posse you will travel across the diverse lands of the Weird West, fighting a variety of enemies and taking part in duels, train robberies, and horseback chases.

In Hard West II, the main focus is on tactical, turn-based combat, in which the player commands a group of gunslingers, each with unique abilities developed through creating special combinations of mysterious cards. An important gameplay element is the Bravado mechanic, which grants additional action points after defeating enemies and allows you to chain your companions’ actions into spectacular kill sequences.

Meta pulls down controversial terms of service

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

All the latest headlines and insights from the world of digital journalism and publishing. Our daily newsletter is a must-read from newsrooms to executive suites.

We will never sell your address. Prefer a weekly email?

We have sent a confirmation link to . Click it and you are on the list. If it has not turned up in a minute or two, it may be in your spam folder.

“We’re pausing the changes as we continue to engage with stakeholders and take our time to get this right.”

Meta has indefinitely delayed its proposed commercial terms of service, which would have permitted the company to scrape the websites of Meta Business account users.

On Thursday, the company took down the preview link to the proposed terms and published an update to its Business Help Center that reads: “We recently shared that we were updating the Meta Commercial Terms, Product Catalog Terms, Self-Serve Ad Terms, and Ad Creative Generative AI Terms. These updates are now paused and will not take effect on November 20, 2026. We are continuing to consult with stakeholders and will provide a further update when we have more to share. In the meantime, our existing terms remain in effect.”

Meta — which owns Facebook, Messenger, Instagram, WhatsApp, and Threads — has repeatedly pushed back the terms’ effective date since Nieman Lab first reported on them on October 15. The terms were first set to take effect on October 30, sparking global outcry among news publishers, politicians, and trade organizations. On October 16, Meta removed the word “train” from its terms and limited the use of future scraped content to “advertising and business products.” On October 23, Meta moved the effective date back by three weeks to November 20.

Last week, Meta pledged to consult news publishers and industry leaders ahead of putting new commercial terms into effect.

“We’ve heard publishers’ feedback on our proposed ads and business terms update. We care deeply about our partners and do not plan to make policy changes without a full consultation with industry groups and leaders,” vice president of communications Andy Stone tweeted on October 28. “We’re committed to getting this right, and as we expect this consultation could extend beyond November 20, in which case we’d delay implementation of the updated terms.”

Since then, courts in Norway and Brazil have issued preliminary injunctions to block the terms from taking effect as a result of lawsuits filed by media organizations in both countries. The United Kingdom’s Competition and Markets Authority has opened a formal investigation into Meta, examining whether the company was “abusing a dominant position by imposing exploitative terms on publishers and advertisers.”

In the United States, the proposed terms stoked bipartisan concern. At the end of October, Sens. Adam Schiff (D-Calif.) and Marsha Blackburn (R-Tenn.) sent letters to Meta CEO Mark Zuckerberg asking him to withdraw the terms.

On this day in space! Nov. 5, 2002: NASA's Galileo spacecraft flies by Jupiter moon Amalthea only to find it's a 'pile of icy rubble less dense than water'

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

The Galileo spacecraft was running on cosmic fumes by the time it reached Amalthea and the flyby would be a last of its kind.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

On Nov. 5, 2002, NASA's old Galileo spacecraft was almost out of fuel. But the Jupiter mission still had some science to do. Galileo flew by a small moon called Amalthea. Amalthea is small, but it's interesting for scientists.

The Jupiter moon Amalthea is the reddest object in our solar system. It also generates more heat than it gets from the sun. Scientists think Amalthea creates electrical currents as it orbits in Jupiter's huge magnetic field. Near this little moon, strong radiation from Jupiter is dangerous for spacecraft.

The flyby caused some problems for Galileo. The spacecraft went into safe mode only 30 minutes after going past Amalthea. NASA said it was ready for problems, because Galileo was on its last legs, and the radiation near Amalthea was strong. However, NASA quickly fixed the problem and got Galileo working again.

Less than a year later, Galileo threw itself into Jupiter's atmosphere. The spacecraft was crushed by the high pressure in Jupiter's atmosphere, but its data still survives on Earth.

The Galileo spacecraft was running on cosmic fumes by the time it reached Amalthea and the flyby would be the last time the probe would ever swing by one of Jupiter's moons.

While it may not look like it in Galileo's black and white images above and below, Amalthea is tinted red due sulfur contamination on its surface. The moon is small, about 168 miles long, and orbits about 112,468 miles (181,000 km) from Jupiter (much closer to the planet than the Earth and its moon).

Galileo flew within 99 miles of Amalthea during its flyby, which also marked the closest Galileo would get to Jupiter before its death dive in September 2005.

"Scientists studying data from NASA's Galileo spacecraft have found that Jupiter's moon Amalthea is a pile of icy rubble less dense than water," NASA announced in May 2005

"Amalthea is throwing us a curve ball," Torrence Johnson, a study co-author on the Almathea flyby and project scientist for the Galileo mission at JPL, said in 2005. "Its density is well below that of water ice, and even with substantial porosity, Amalthea probably contains a lot of water ice, as well as rock."

Originally known as Jupiter V (because it was the fifth Jupiter moon discovered after the so-called Galilean moons Io, Europa, Ganymede, Callisto), Amalthea was named after naiad, or water nymph, from Greek mythology.

It is fitting that Amalthea was the last of Jupiter's moons to be visited by the Galileo spacecraft. The moon was also last Jupiter moon to be discovered by visual observations when it was first disovered by astronomer Edward Emerson Barnard in September 1892 using the Lick Observatory telescope in California.

The Galileo spacecraft ultimately crashed into Jupiter on Sept. 21, 2005, less than a year after its Amalthea flyby.

After studying physics at the University of Tennessee in her hometown of Knoxville, she earned her graduate degree in Science, Health and Environmental Reporting (SHERP) from New York University. Here is Why the Crypto Market Erased Over $120 Billion Today

The crypto market cap declined by more than $120 billion on Thursday to $2.57 trillion.

Bitcoin, ETH, XRP, and Solana led the broader cryptocurrency selloff over the past 24 hours.

Despite the renewed selling pressure on Thursday, a possible recovery is anticipated in the near future

As Bitcoin (BTC), Ethereum (ETH), XRP, and Solana (SOL) capitulated on November 5, 2026, the crypto market capitalization flash-crashed by more than $120 billion.

The crypto market cap fell to $2.57 trillion at the time of reporting, down from $2.69 trillion on Wednesday. As a result, the total cryptocurrency market cap declined by roughly 4.46%, according to data Coinpedia analyzed from CoinMarketCap.

Crypto market cap change on November 5. Source: CoinMarketCap

The free-fall was driven by a significant selloff in top crypto assets. At the time of writing, Bitcoin, ETH, XRP, and SOL had shed 4.46%, 6.68%, 4.45%, and 5.9%, respectively.

Top 7 crypto assets by market cap on Thursday. Source: CoinMarketCap

The cryptocurrency market faced heightened selling pressure following renewed geopolitical tensions amid a broader bear market in the artificial intelligence (AI) stocks.

Over the past 24 hours, the Nasdaq-100 Index (NDX), an equity benchmark tracking the 100 largest non-financial companies listed on the Nasdaq Stock Market, plummeted to its lowest in 196 days.

As such, the cryptocurrency market faced increased selling pressure catalyzed by broader macro headwinds.

Most importantly, the crypto market experienced a long squeeze, a situation in which falling prices force long holders to sell, thereby accelerating the decline. Over the past 24 hours, the cryptocurrencies’ derivatives had recorded around $1.06 billion in total liquidations, with $934.73 million involving long trades, as per metrics from CoinGlass.

Cryptocurrency liquidation heat map on Thursday. Source: CoinGlass

Even though the cryptocurrency market faced increased selloff fueled by a possible AI bubble burst, a possible bull run is expected before the end of this year.

Disbelief: This rally will fail like the others. https://t.co/I01R9xzNdT pic.twitter.com/Elso1woPUa

Crypto analyst Ali Martinez believes the recent downtrend marked the ‘disbelief’ part of the bull cycle, thus signaling a potential recovery in the near term.

How Psychological Flexibility Can Help Us Belong

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

The most trusted resource for mindfulness and meditation.

Six ways to move toward connection—even when you feel anxious, awkward, or left out.

The key to belonging may be more within your reach than you think. You don’t need to become more interesting, more confident, or less awkward. You may simply need to become more psychologically flexible.

Psychological flexibility is your capacity to stay present, open to your inner experience, and engaged with what matters to you—even when things feel uncomfortable. Researchers have been studying this capacity for decades, and it turns out that when you are more psychologically flexible, you are not only less likely to struggle with anxiety and depression, you are also more capable of pursuing what matters to you. That might mean repairing  strained relationships, joining the exercise class you feel anxious about, or starting the book club you know would bring more conversation and connection into your life.

Our sense of belonging may be shaped, at least in part, by how we respond to the difficult thoughts and feelings that arise when we reach for connection.

A recent study of more than 16,000 undergraduate students at 104 U.S. colleges and universities found that greater psychological flexibility was associated with a stronger sense of belonging. The study does not prove that flexibility causes belonging, but it points to something hopeful: Our sense of belonging may be shaped, at least in part, by how we respond to the difficult thoughts and feelings that arise when we reach for connection.

Emotional flexibility is your capacity to make room for the range of feelings that can come with being part of a group—whether you’re feeling left out, awkward, or nervous—without becoming overwhelmed by those emotions or needing to suppress them.

Meeting new people can feel awkward, getting past small talk can be tedious, and encountering people with different views can sometimes be irritating.

Sometimes our emotions give us important information. Maybe you are dreading that work event because there’s heavy drinking, and it doesn’t align with your values. That discomfort may be worth listening to. At other times, feeling uncomfortable is simply part of the experience. Meeting new people can feel awkward, getting past small talk can be tedious, and encountering people with different views can sometimes be irritating.

Try this small emotional flexibility practice: Imagine placing the feelings you’re having about a social situation into a backpack. How do you want to carry them? Do you want to shift the weight from side to side? Find someone to help you carry it?  Or perhaps offer yourself some self compassion along the way? You may not get to decide what is inside the backpack, but you can decide how you carry it into your next interaction.

Every so often, look inside. Do these feelings have useful information for you? Do they  need care or attention? If not, adjust the straps and bring them along.

Your mind can be the world’s worst co-pilot when it comes to social situations. It compares you to others, judges you, makes negative predictions and sometimes is just plain mean.

Cognitive defusion is the practice of stepping back from our thoughts rather than automatically treating them as facts.

In Acceptance and Commitment Therapy (ACT), cognitive defusion is the practice of stepping back from our thoughts rather than automatically treating them as facts.

Before entering a social event, write down some of the thoughts you’re having. Just noting a thought is a thought, helps you get some space from it. You get to decide if you want to take your mind’s advice, or just say thank you and move on.

Try this  cognitive defusion practice: When unhelpful thoughts return, try responding: “Thank you, mind. I know you are trying to help me. And I’ve decided to do this.”

The goal is not to get rid of the thoughts. It is to stop allowing them to make all your social decisions.

When we feel as though we don’t belong, we often see ourselves through a narrow lens: I am not good at conversation. I am the only person who doesn’t know someone here. I never make a good first impression.

You know you are limiting yourself when you use phrases like: I am, I always, I never, I am the only one who…

You know you are limiting yourself when you use phrases like:

I am, I always, I never, I am the only one who…

These kinds of statements can make a passing experience feel like a permanent part of who we are. But pretty much everyone has felt socially awkward at one time or another, and nearly everyone longs to belong. And chances are, you aren’t always this way.

Try this self-flexibility practice: When you notice a  narrow view of yourself taking over, see whether you can become more flexible with your idea of “me.” Can you notice someone else who may be feeling similarly? Can you recognize a way that you are already connected—through your nervousness, or simply by sharing this moment? And can you see yourself as someone who isn’t defined by  “always” and “never”? What changes when you make room for sometimes instead?

In ACT, values describe how you want to show up in your life. They are qualities of action— such as courageously, warmly, playfully, honestly, or presently.

Before entering a social situation, try this values practice. Ask yourself:

If I already felt like I fit in, how would I act?

These questions help you identify how you want to participate and connect with others. You don’t have to wait until you feel confident or included. In fact, the more you act in alignment with your values, the more you may begin to experience yourself as someone who already belongs.

Social anxiety has a way of pulling our attention inward. It shines a spotlight on how we look, whether we’re stumbling over our words, or what might go wrong next.

Try being more flexible with your attention. Notice your feet on the floor, listen to what the other person has to say, or take in the group as a whole. Shifting your focus like this can help you become less caught up in self-consciousness and more aware of what’s happening around you. There’s more to the scene than the words you are saying.

Try this attentional flexibility practice: When you notice yourself getting caught up in comparison or self-judgment, try shifting your attention. What are five things you see? What are four things you hear? What are three things you care about? What are two people you want to meet? What is one thing that matters most to you right now?

When you feel the longing to belong, try acting on it in a small way.

We build belonging one small connection at a time.

When you feel the longing to belong, try acting on it in a small way. At the grocery store, strike up a conversation with the person grabbing lettuce next to you (“What salad are you making?”) At school pickup, get out of your car and wait with other caregivers (“Who’s your kid?”). If you are waiting for a yoga class to start, talk to the person next to you (“Have taken this class before?”) If  someone is sharing the space with you, you already have something in common. It takes courage to make that move, but small gestures like these can open the door to meaningful connections over time.

Use your other psychological flexibility skills to support you! Make room for discomfort, step back from unhelpful thoughts, shift your attention away from self-judgement, broaden your perspective,  and remember why you want to connect in the first place.

Psychological flexibility offers you a greater range of ways to respond, even when you feel like you don’t quite fit in. You can make room for anxiety, step back from your mind’s commentary, widen your sense of self, orient toward your values, return to the present, and make one small move toward connection.

Try out some of these skills, and you will notice, belonging is something you co-create, one flexible move at a time.

Diana Hill, PhD is a clinical psychologist, international trainer and sought-out speaker on Acceptance and Commitment Therapy (ACT) and compassion. Host of the Wise Effort podcast, author of The Self-Compassion Daily Journal, and co-author the ACT Daily Journal. @drdianahill

Amex Platinum Card Review: Benefits & Credits That Easily Justify The Fee

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

While premium cards are incredibly popular nowadays, this is really a market that Amex started, with the American Express Platinum Card®. It takes some effort to maximize the card’s perks, but if you’re willing to put in the time, the value is potentially incredible.

This is my in-depth Amex Platinum Card review, so let me talk about the card’s value proposition, and the perks that make the Amex Platinum worthwhile. This is a card you should get for the rich benefits that it offers, including the lounge access, credits, and elite status, and not necessarily a card you should spend a lot of money on under normal circumstances, since there are more rewarding cards for that.

Link: Learn more about the American Express Platinum Card®

Before I get into my full Amex Platinum Card review, I want to go over the card’s key details at a glance. The card has such a long list of statement credits that it’s impossible to cover those briefly, so keep reading below to see those, as they could be worth up to thousands of dollars annually. To keep the list to non-statement credit details, here’s what you can expect:

I also put together my pros and cons of the Amex Platinum Card:

The Amex Platinum Card potentially has a huge welcome offer, though the card also has a high annual fee. So let’s cover all those details first, including card eligibility, since these are major factors cardmembers consider when applying for a card.

The Amex Platinum Card currently has the following lucrative welcome bonus offer: You may be eligible for as high as 175,000 Membership Rewards® Points after spending $12,000 in eligible purchases on your new Card in your first 6 months of Membership. Welcome offers vary and you may not be eligible for an offer.

Welcome offers vary and you may not be eligible for an offer, so you’ll want to start the application, and then you’ll see what kind of an offer is available to you.

Based on my latest miles & points valuations, Membership Rewards points are worth 1.7 cents each, so depending on the offer you’re eligible for, this could be worth almost $3,000 in value. That’s pretty stellar, especially for a card that offers so much ongoing value after your first year.

The welcome bonus on the Amex Platinum Card isn’t available to those who currently have the card, or those who have had the card in the past (so the card has a “once in a lifetime” rule). If you’ve had the card in the past you’re still eligible to be approved, you just won’t get the bonus.

Note that this is a hybrid card and not a credit card, meaning that having this card won’t count toward Amex’s limit of five credit cards per person. Make sure you consider all of Amex’s standard application restrictions before applying.

Eligibility for this card is otherwise unrelated to having any other type of card that’s not an Amex Platinum, so lots of people should be eligible for the offer.

The Amex Platinum Card has a $895 annual fee. Even among premium cards, this is among the highest annual fees out there, though for many people, the perks will more than offset the fee.

If you want to add authorized users to the card, you’ll pay $195 each (Rates & Fees). Fortunately there are some solid perks for authorized users, including lounge access, so some people might find that adding them is worthwhile.

As you’d expect of a premium travel card, the Amex Platinum has no foreign transaction fees (Rates & Fees). This means you can use it abroad without racking up fees, though it’s not usually the best card for maximizing everyday spending on categories like dining.

Personally, I think the Amex Platinum Card is worth having for the benefits it offers, though it’s not a card that I think is worth spending much money on, with limited exceptions.

Some other Amex cards offer big bonus categories. For example, the American Express® Gold Card (learn more) offers 4x Membership Rewards points at restaurants worldwide (on up to $50,000 in purchases per calendar year, and then 1x points) and at U.S. supermarkets (on up to $25,000 of spending per calendar year, and then 1x points), which is extremely lucrative.

Unfortunately, the Amex Platinum Card isn’t as generous in that regard. However, there are still some useful bonus categories…

The best bonus category on the Amex Platinum Card is 5x points on airfare purchased directly from airlines or through American Express Travel® (on up to $500,000 of flight purchases per calendar year, and then 1x points). This is spectacular, and it makes it the best card for airfare purchases, in my opinion.

Officially booking through online travel agencies (except amextravel.com) wouldn’t earn you bonus points, though in practice it may still sometimes code for 5x points. That’s because often online travel agencies still have the airline bill you directly when you book flights through them.

I value Membership Rewards points at 1.7 cents each, so to me, that’s the equivalent of an 8.5% return on airfare spending, which is incredible.

The Amex Platinum Card also offers 5x Membership Rewards points on prepaid hotels that are booked through Amex Travel. As a general rule of thumb, this isn’t worth it. That’s because you’re usually not earning loyalty points with your preferred hotel program when you book through Amex Travel, so that’s quite an opportunity cost.

The exception is Amex Fine Hotels + Resorts®, where prepaid reservations earn 5x points. That can be a good deal because of the perks you get, and also because you still earn points with your preferred hotel loyalty program. You can also combine this with the card’s annual hotel credit to really maximize value. I’ll talk more about that in a bit.

The above are the only bonus categories — otherwise, the Amex Platinum Card offers 1x points per dollar spent, which isn’t especially good. There are other cards offering 1.5x-2x points per dollar spent, so I would much rather put my spending on those cards, or on another card that’s best for everyday spending.

The Amex Platinum Card earns Membership Rewards points. This is one of the major transferable points currencies, and is a favorite points currency for many. While I love Membership Rewards, I’d say that not everyone should be earning these points.

The best way to redeem your Amex points is to transfer them to one of the Membership Rewards airline or hotel partners. Points can be transferred to the below airline partners and hotel partners.

The reason this represents such a good option is that you can get outsized value toward premium airfare, especially for travel in first and business class. These tickets are often super expensive if paying cash, while they can be a good deal with points.

For example, I frequently transfer my Amex Membership Rewards points to Air France-KLM, and then redeem 60,000 points for business class across the Atlantic. Best of all, there are sometimes transfer bonuses of up to 25-30%, which lowers my real “out of pocket” in terms of Amex points even further.

I’d note that while you can redeem Membership Rewards points toward the cost of merchandise or gift cards, or to pay off your statement, these aren’t good uses of your points. You’ll typically get less than one cent of value per point, so that’s not how I’d redeem them.

I’d recommend earning Membership Rewards points if your primary interest is transferring points to partner programs.

The Amex Platinum Card really shines when it comes to its credits, as the card potentially offers thousands of dollars in annual credits. As you can imagine, for some people this alone will more than justify the annual fee. The catch is that there are some hurdles to using all of these, so don’t consider most of these credits to be worth face value.

However, I’d argue that it’s quite easy to recoup your entire annual fee on these credits alone. So let’s take a look at what you need to know about these credits, and how you can maximize them. (Enrollment is required for select benefits)

The Amex Platinum Card offers up to $600 per year in hotel credits. This is a semi-annual credit, so you get a $300 credit in January through June, and a $300 credit in July through December.

This is actually a pretty easy perk to use — just book a prepaid hotel through Amex Fine Hotels + Resorts® (no minimum stay) or The Hotel Collection (two night minimum stay) through American Express Travel®, and you can receive up to a $300 credit semi-annually. Effective January 1, 2027, Fine Hotels + Resorts® bookings will require a minimum two-night stay to be eligible for the Hotel Credit.

If you ask me, this perk alone can help recoup most of the card’s annual fee. So a major point in deciding whether this card makes sense is crunching the numbers as to whether you can get full value from this.

The Amex Platinum Card offers up to $400 per year in Resy credits, and enrollment is required. This is a quarterly credit, so the idea is that you receive up to $100 worth of statement credits each calendar quarter toward dining at eligible U.S. Resy restaurants.

There are no hoops you have to jump through, so as long as you dine at an eligible Resy restaurant and pay with your card, you can receive the statement credit. Personally, I’d consider this benefit to be worth close to face value.

The Amex Platinum Card offers up to $300 per year in digital entertainment credits, with enrollment being required. This comes in the form of a $25 monthly statement credit on eligible purchases or subscriptions with any of the following:

I’d consider this to be quite valuable, since there’s an impressive variety of partners with which you can maximize this perk.

The Amex Platinum Card offers up to $300 per year in Lululemon credits, with enrollment being required. This is a quarterly credit, so the idea is that you receive up to $75 worth of statement credits each calendar quarter toward purchases at Lululemon stores in the U.S. If you’re in the market for some athleisure wear, this is a great option.

The Amex Platinum Card offers up to $300 per year in Equinox credits, and enrollment is required. This comes in the form of up to $300 in statement credits each year on a digital or club membership at Equinox. If you’d otherwise pay for an Equinox membership, this is a great perk, while otherwise, it’s probably of limited value.

The Amex Platinum Card offers a $300 credit when purchasing a SoulCycle bike. This credit can be used toward the purchase of a SoulCycle at-home bike, and it can be used for the purchase of up to 15 bikes per year.

You need to make the entire purchase of a bike in one transaction on your Amex Platinum Card in order to qualify for these savings. Suffice to say that this will be of limited value to most cardmembers.

The Amex Platinum Card offers up to $219 per year in CLEAR credits. When using your card to pay for a CLEAR membership, you’ll receive statement credits of up to $219 per calendar year. For those not familiar with CLEAR, this company can save you time at security, by allowing you to use your biometric data to skip the ID check.

The Amex Platinum Card offers up to $200 per year in airline fee credits, which can be used toward airline incidentals and fees. The credit is offered every calendar year, so it follows a strict January 1 through December 31 calendar.

Every year you can designate on which airline you want to receive the credit, of the following:

You can change your airline selection in January of each year. If you don’t change your selection, then you automatically keep the same airline choice as the previous year.

Unfortunately, you can’t use your credit toward the following:

Airline tickets, upgrades, mileage points purchases, mileage points transfer fees, gift cards, duty free purchases, and award tickets are not deemed to be incidental fees.

This is particularly useful for airline ticket change fees (of which there are few nowadays), baggage fees, and other costs you may incur with an airline. Personally, this is something I have no trouble maxing out.

The Amex Platinum Card offers up to $200 per year in Oura Ring credits. This is valid if you use your card to purchase an Oura Ring through ouraring.com, and enrollment is required. I’d say this is very niche, but if you’re in the market for this product, it has the potential to be valuable.

The Amex Platinum Card offers up to $200 per year in Uber credits. The way this is broken up, you get a $15 credit per month, and then in December, you get a total of $35 in credits. You can use this either for rides with Uber in the US, or for food with Uber Eats in the US.

To activate this benefit, you need to enter your Amex Platinum Card card number on your Uber account, and then you should see the benefit unlocked. You’ll see an amount added to your Uber Cash balance every month, and the discount will automatically be applied to the first eligible ride or food delivery.

An Amex Card must be selected as the payment method for your Uber or Uber Eats transaction to redeem the Amex Uber Cash benefit

The Amex Platinum Card offers up to $120 in statement credits each calendar year after purchasing an auto-renewing Uber One membership with the card. This is separate from the card’s $200 annual Uber Cash benefit.

An Uber One membership offers all kinds of benefits for rides with Uber and deliveries with Uber Eats, so if you use the platform with any frequency, this is pretty valuable.

The Amex Platinum Card offers up to $12.95 per month in credits toward a Walmart+ membership, which covers the full membership cost. Enrollment is required. (Up to $12.95 plus applicable local sales tax. Plus Ups not eligible.) With Walmart+, you can receive free deliveries on groceries or get savings at gas stations or on video streaming.

The Amex Platinum Card offers a Global Entry or TSA PreCheck statement credit every four years. Simply charge the membership fee to your card, and it will be automatically reimbursed. It’s worth getting Global Entry, which has a $120 application fee compared to PreCheck’s $85, because it includes TSA PreCheck membership too.

It doesn’t matter who the fee is being paid for, as long as you charge it to your eligible card.

The Amex Platinum Card offers one of the most comprehensive lounge access benefits of any card. This is called the Global Lounge Collection, and it offers access to over 1,550 airport lounges across 140 countries for eligible cardmembers. This includes proprietary Amex lounges, as well as access to lounges run by other airlines and companies.

Before I go through the details, let me mention that Lufthansa lounge access has been cut as of October 1, 2026, which is why I’m not including it in the list, though that used to be a benefit.

Amex is well known for its Centurion Lounges, and the Amex Platinum Card can get you into these lounges. These are significantly better than what you’ll find in most U.S. airline lounges, with a solid selection of hot food and cocktails.

Currently, U.S. locations include Atlanta, Charlotte, Dallas, Denver, Houston, Las Vegas, Los Angeles, Miami, New York JFK, New York LaGuardia, Philadelphia, Phoenix, San Francisco, Seattle, and Washington, with more to come. There are also Centurion Lounges in Amsterdam, Hong Kong, London, and Tokyo, among other international destinations.

You can access these lounges up to three hours before your flight’s departure (if you’re connecting, then the three-hour limit doesn’t apply). Please note that you’re only eligible to bring in guests on a complimentary basis if you spend at least $75,000 per calendar year on the card.

The Amex Platinum Card also offers access to the Delta Sky Club, with some limitations. Card members can receive access within three hours of a departing or arriving flight, and must present their card, a boarding pass showing a confirmed reservation for an eligible Delta flight, and a government-issued ID.

Eligible cards issued in the United States receive 10 visits to the Delta Sky Club each year, to be used from February 1 until January 31 of the next calendar year, with all visits within a 24-hour period counting as a single visit. It’s also possible to earn an unlimited number of visits by spending $75,000 in eligible purchases on a card account in a calendar year.

The Amex Platinum Card offers a Priority Pass™ membership, which gives you access to lounges around the world. You can bring up to two guests into these lounges. While these aren’t the most exciting and high quality lounges, there’s no denying that this gives you access to a comprehensive collection of lounges.

The Amex Platinum Card offers access to Plaza Premium Lounges. Just present your Amex Platinum Card, and you’ll be admitted, and you can even bring one guest with you at no additional cost. This is hugely valuable, as Plaza Premium has dozens of lounges around the world, which are among the highest quality contract lounges you’ll find.

The Amex Platinum Card offers access to the growing network of Escape Lounges. Just present your Amex Platinum Card and you’ll be admitted, and you can even bring in guests.

Beyond the annual credits that potentially more than justify the annual fee, as well as an incredible lounge access program, the Amex Platinum Card also offers status with three hotel loyalty programs, status with three rental car loyalty programs, access to a program that gets you valuable hotel perks, and discounts on many premium cabin airline tickets. (Enrollment is required for select benefits)

Those with the Amex Platinum Card receive complimentary Hilton Gold elite status for as long as they have the card. This status is useful, and offers the following perks:

Those with the Amex Platinum Card receive complimentary Marriott Gold status for as long as they have the card, which offers the following perks, among other things:

Those with the Amex Platinum Card receive Leaders Club Sterling status from Leading Hotels of the World. Leading Hotels of the World is a collection of independent, luxury properties, so this offers perks at some properties that don’t otherwise participate in major global hotel groups.

This status typically requires $5,000 in annual spending at these hotels and offers perks such as five pre-arrival upgrades per year.

The Amex Platinum Card offers Hertz President’s Circle elite status, which is Hertz’s top-tier status. Your status is valid as long as you remain a cardmember; you just need to enroll. This should take the pain out of renting cars and offers the following perks:

The Amex Platinum Card offers National Emerald Club Executive status. You have to enroll to take advantage of this, and it’s valid for as long as you have your card. National is my favorite rental car company, and Executive status lets you pick out your car from the lot, and skip the counter altogether.

The Amex Platinum also offers Avis Preferred Plus status. Once you enroll, you’ll get this mid-tier status level with the rental car company and qualify for complimentary upgrades when available, a 25% points bonus, and a dedicated reservation and customer service line. You also get access to a discount code that can save you up to 25% off qualifying rentals.

With the Platinum Member Airfares program (formerly known as the International Airline Program), you can save on the cost of international first class, business class, and premium economy airfare, on around two dozen airlines. This typically provides discounts even on already discounted tickets, so is a spectacular value.

If you book paid international premium cabin travel with any frequency, this could cover the card’s annual fee over and over.

With the Amex Fine Hotels + Resorts® program, you can receive extra perks for stays at over 1,900 luxury hotels around the world. These benefits include the following:

Best of all, the rate is typically in line with the best available rate. Having the Amex Platinum Card gets you access to this, and if you prepay your reservation, you even earn 5x points. You can even stack this with the card’s up to $600 annual hotel credit.

The Amex Platinum Card offers several travel insurance benefits that can add extra peace of mind. You’ll want to consult the card’s benefits guide for the exact terms and any caps and exclusions, but here’s a quick overview:

Up to $500 per Covered Trip that is delayed for more than 6 hours; and 2 claims per Eligible Card per 12 consecutive month period. Eligibility and Benefit level varies by Card. The card also offers an extended warranty protection benefit as well as purchase, return, and cell phone protection.

What benefits do authorized users on the Amex Platinum Card get? They receive most of the same perks as primary cardmembers.

This includes all the lounge access perks, access to Amex Fine Hotels + Resorts®, Hilton and Marriott Gold status, Leading Hotels of the World Sterling status, access to the Amex International Airline Program, additional Global Entry/TSA PreCheck credits, and more.

The main thing that authorized users aren’t getting is incremental credits, so they’re not receiving extra airline fee credits, digital entertainment credits, Uber credits, etc.

Of course there’s never going to be any “one size fits all” answer as to whether a card makes sense, since we all value perks differently. However, speaking only for myself, I’d argue that the Amex Platinum Card is rather easy to justify.

To start, let’s just take a look at the credits. Personally, I’m able to get full value out of the $600 annual hotel credit, $400 annual Resy credit, $300 annual digital entertainment credit, $200 annual airline fee credit, and $200 annual Uber credit. That’s $1,700 in value right there, and it actually doesn’t take much effort for me to maximize that.

That also says nothing of all the other perks offered by the card, from the bonus points on airfare, to the lounge access, and more. So yeah, I’m really finding this card to be pretty great. Let me emphasize that in 2027 the hotel credit will be harder to maximize, so I’ll need to assess how much value I get out of that. But even if you drop that benefit entirely, I’m still getting $1,100 in value from the other credits.

Everyone has to crunch the numbers for themselves, but I think the math works out very nicely on this product. But that’s not the case for everyone. So who should skip the Amex Platinum Card?

If it won’t be easy for you to get full value out of the card’s many travel and statement credits — or even if you just don’t want to be troubled with keeping track of so many credits to prevent leaving value on the table — the Amex Platinum may not be the card for you. Credit card fatigue is a real problem!

It’s also not a great card for everyday spending, since it doesn’t earn bonus rewards on categories like dining. I’ve written a more detailed post specifically addressing the question of whether the Amex Platinum is worth it, so I recommend checking that out.

This also brings me to the next section, which is the best Amex Platinum alternatives.

There are several premium cards to choose from nowadays, so it always makes sense to look at what the competition is offering. Let’s look at a few popular alternatives to the Amex Platinum.

The Chase Sapphire Reserve® (learn more) is the other premium card heavy hitter, with a slightly lower annual fee of $795. I have a full post comparing the Amex Platinum and Chase Sapphire Reserve — my verdict is that both cards are worth having, but if you can only pick one, the Sapphire Reserve is better for lazy optimizers while the Amex Platinum is better for those willing to put some real work into optimizing all the card’s benefits.

Here’s an overview of the Chase Sapphire Reserve’s standout features. It’s no slouch when it comes to perks, but its list of benefits and statement credits is considerably shorter than the Amex Platinum Card’s. Specifically:

If you take full advantage of the Amex Platinum’s main statement credits for travel and dining, it has the potential to offer more value than the Chase Sapphire Reserve. But the Chase Sapphire Reserve’s earning rates are better for maximizing everyday purchases, and you might prefer having fewer “coupon book” style benefits to keep track of.

In my opinion, the Amex Gold Card (learn more) is one of the most rewarding cards out there. At $325 (Rates & Fees), its annual fee is significantly lower than the Amex Platinum Card’s, and while it doesn’t have as many benefits, it’s a better choice for everyday spending, especially if you eat out frequently.

Here are the highlights of the Amex Gold:

The Amex Gold Card can’t beat the Platinum when it comes to travel perks, but if your main goal is earning as many Amex points as possible on your spending, it could be a more useful everyday pick.

The Capital One Venture X Rewards Credit Card (learn more) is another premium card with an annual fee that’s one step down from the Amex Platinum — in this case, it’s $395. I find this card to offer strong value, and it’s much simpler than the Amex Platinum Card:

The travel credit and anniversary bonus miles help justify the annual fee, and while 2x miles isn’t the highest everyday spending rate, it’s a better base than 1x points with the Amex Platinum. It’s also worth comparing Amex points with Capital One miles to see which currency you prefer before making a final decision.

The Amex Platinum Card has a $895 annual fee and a much longer list of credits than it used to. This isn’t a card that you should get for the return on spending it offers, but instead, it’s a card to get for all of the perks and credits that it offers.

While the $895 annual fee is obviously steep, personally I’m able to get outsized value thanks to all the credits offered, which offer significant value. And that says nothing of the bonus points on airfare, the lounge access, and more.

So even if you haven’t been able to make the math on this card work in the past, it might be time to give it another look.

Combined OpenSSH and musl-libc flaws let anyone into Alpine Linux and other distros by typing anything over 256 characters as a password

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Get Tom's Hardware's best news and in-depth reviews, straight to your inbox.

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

Meanwhile, the parties involved are playing a game of hot potato with who's fault.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

The internet is still reeling from the PHP engine vulnerability and all other security vulnerabilities found over the past few weeks, and we're not done. Today's special is quite spicy: you can get into Alpine, OpenWRT, and other Linux distros without needing a password, thanks to a combined bug with OpenSSH and musl libc. Just randomly type over 256 characters on the password prompt and you'll be let in through the SSHhole.

It works if you set an empty password for system users, which is the default on multiple distros. The bug is present, though not necessarily enabled, across Buildroot, Yocto, and Void Linux. OpenWRT uses Dropbear for authentication by default, but users can install OpenSSH instead.

The largest concern is by far Alpine, though, as it's pretty much the default choice for what could amount to millions of installs across Docker/Kubernetes/Proxmox containers and endless dependencies thereof, servers, cloud VMs, and embedded devices, among many others. Alpine comes with the requisite conditions set and password authentication enabled by default, which is why it needs immediate patching.

Broadly speaking, the vulnerability combination is: OpenSSH, musl, password authentication not explicitly disabled, not using PAM, and the one arbitrary user with a password set, using a SHA-512/256 hash. Patch your systems immediately, as per the project's advisory note. Meanwhile, the easiest mitigation is explicitly setting "PasswordAuthentication" to "no" in your OpenSSH configuration file and restarting the service.

While in practice the vast majority of Alpine and similar installs aren't reachable from the internet, any attacker inside a network, including but not limited to corporate setups, home labs, cloud networks, and VPNs, can let themselves in. Likewise, all it takes is one valid user account, not too hard to get in a large enough corporate environment, and then an attacker is inside the network. Scanning and testing the vulnerability is also trivial.

OpenSSH has already published a fix and an advisory post, as did musl author Rich Felker. Both parties seem to believe the other is at fault, but looking from the outside, blame is shared.

If you've ever looked in the /etc/passwd or /etc/shadow files, you may have noticed a single asterisk as a placeholder for the password hash for accounts with no password — meaning "no password should work." The SSHhole website has all the details, but the snafu happens both when musl's crypt() function returns "*" as an error condition instead of a null value as per usual conventions, and then OpenSSH uses it to compare to a password hash, when it shouldn't. An overly long input then triggers the chain of mistakes.

PixelMeter Now Lets You Compare Specs and Scores on Cameras and Lenses

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

PixelMeter Now Lets You Compare Specs and Scores on Cameras and Lenses

PixelMeter, the first and only review aggregator for cameras, lenses, smartphones, and more, has been updated with the most-requested feature since it launched last month: the ability to directly compare products.

“When we created PixelMeter, our goal was to give photographers and videographers a place to quickly gauge how reviewers across the internet feel about a camera or lens at a glance, while still offering easy access to full reviews for those wanting deeper context behind a rating,” Michael Zhang, publisher and founder of PetaPixel, says.

“As many readers pointed out, the next logical step was enabling side-by-side comparisons—allowing users to compare gear head-to-head, review consensus on pros and cons, and evaluate key specifications to make informed decisions much more easily. We now deliver that.”

The new comparison tool can be accessed in a couple of ways. First, while on any PixelMeter category page, an option to “Compare” will appear in the upper left-hand corner when hovering over any product listing. Clicking this option will start a comparison page, and users can then add more products to the comparison as they see fit.

Comparisons can also be started from any PixelMeter product page, directly to the right of the product name.

Camera and lens specifications are extremely tricky to get 100% correct, as specifications vary in how they are presented even on dealer product pages. If there is an issue with a particular specification, you can suggest a correction (via an option right above the specifications section on any product page), and we’ll double-check the issue and make any necessary edits. We greatly appreciate your help in making sure PixelMeter is a reliable, useful resource.

PixelMeter is still growing and improving. We encourage users to suggest products to be added (this can be done by clicking the small + icon right above the “What Reviewers Say” section of any product page). We also welcome feedback on the platform, so please leave comments below with any suggestions or bugs that come up while using PixelMeter.

We’d love to hear what feature you would like to see next on PixelMeter! Let us know what you think in the comments below. Also, as a reminder, PetaPixel Members can browse PixelMeter with no ads ever.

October crypto-tracker hack: Access cracked, law enforcement tokens packed

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Agentic 'on-path attack' went after Chainalysis's tools, which are routinely called upon to assist police investigations

Blockchain analysis outfit Chainalysis has confirmed that when attackers broke into its systems in October, they managed to grab usernames, password hashes, and live tokens.

In an update written late Wednesday evening, the company, a leader in blockchain analysis which has some very high-profile global customers and partners, confirmed the impact of the attack for the first time.

In previous updates, which Chainalysis has issued regularly since it detected the intrusion on October 30, it confirmed that user IDs, full names, email addresses, and organizational role labels were exposed. The news of password hashes and live tokens also being accessed reflects a worsening picture for the company.

Chainalysis did not confirm the type of hashing algorithm it used to encrypt users' passwords, which makes it difficult to draw conclusions about the offline crackability of users' hashed credentials.

Some users received emails instructing them to change their passwords, but Chainalysis said in its latest update that the password reset functionality is currently down. Nevertheless, users were also encouraged to change their passwords on any site where they reuse credentials.

In addition to the exposed data, Chainalysis also detected "abnormal activity" on its transaction screening products, and has taken services down while "our team completes critical work."

These include, but are not limited to, the Reactor blockchain investigation platform used by international law enforcement customers, the Data Solutions threat intelligence platform, and the KYT (know your transaction) anti-money laundering solution's user interface and login page.

Regarding Reactor and the associated investigation data, Chainalysis said: "At this time, we continue to have no evidence that any investigations data has been accessed or Chainalysis attribution data has been manipulated."

The company plans to reinstate KYT and the risk-intelligence Address Screening tool at some point today (Thursday November 5).

Customers will have to provide the company’s support team or their account manager with trusted IP addresses, which will be added to an allowlist. From there, users can begin resetting their passwords.

"We are working around the clock to harden our systems and bring Reactor and Data Solutions back online," Chainalysis said. "We will require additional protections to access accounts, including that every user will have to reset passwords and rotate API keys as appropriate. We will communicate these changes as necessary.

"We know how business-critical these services are to our customers, and we are working expeditiously alongside our outside experts to restore them securely."

Chainalysis has not yet commented on who was behind the attack, but implied that the culprits used an AI agent, and likened the attack to others that have hit "many other tech platforms in recent weeks."

"This month, Chainalysis detected an external actor using offensive agentic techniques to gain unauthorized access to Chainalysis systems and internal tools," it told customers in its initial disclosure on October 30.

"Our teams have been working around the clock to investigate unauthorized activity, patch vulnerabilities, and harden our systems."

In Wednesday's updates, it referred to the intrusion as an "ongoing multifront cyberattack," and later, an "on-path attack," otherwise known as an attacker-in-the-middle (AitM) attack, without elaborating further about what it entailed.

Per its own company description, Chainalysis counts government agencies, cryptocurrency exchanges, financial institutions, and cybersecurity companies among its customer base.

It is one of the world's foremost blockchain analysis companies and is routinely engaged to assist in cyber-related law enforcement investigations.

Recent cases the company was asked to support include the takedowns of crypto-laundering network AudiA6 and bulletproof hosting provider Virtualine, both of which were known facilitators of ransomware crews.

It also played a role in bringing down the infamous LockBit ransomware operation, among other engagements in the interim. ®

900-Acre Wildfire in White Mountains Likely Caused by Humans, Authorities Say

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

An unusually large wildfire in the White Mountain National Forest near Gorham, New Hampshire, has spread to nearly 856 acres and is 11% contained as of the most recent update. The Wild River Fire was first reported on November 1 in the steep, rugged terrain south of Gorham and spread rapidly, fueled by drought conditions. No residential structures are currently threatened.

While the fire’s cause is still under investigation, a Forest Service spokesperson told New Hampshire Public Radio that experts believe it was caused by human activity.

A closure order is in effect in the area around the Wild River Fire, as shown on the map below. The Appalachian Trail passes northwest of the fire and remains open, although the Appalachian Trail Conservancy urged hikers in the area to use caution, follow all instructions from fire crews and posted signs, and be aware they may see and smell smoke. The organization also cautioned hikers that the Kenduskeag Trail, which connects to the AT at northbound mile 1,894.8, is closed.

As recently as yesterday, the fire was estimated at nearly 1,000 acres, but that estimate shrank today as a result of updated mapping. However, 856 acres is still an unprecedented size for a wildfire in this region. “That’s very uncommon in our area,” Gorham Fire Chief Philip Cloutier told NH Public Radio on Wednesday. “The largest wildfire that we can remember was just under 100 acres, and that was over 20 years ago.”

Despite dry conditions on the ground and challenging terrain, the Forest Service said in a press release that firefighters are making steady progress on suppression efforts. “I am proud of the firefighters who responded quickly and continue to work tirelessly to contain the Wild River Fire,” noted Josh Sjostrom, District Ranger for the White Mountain National Forest’s Androscoggin Ranger District. “We will continue to work with our neighbors and local and state partners to contain this fire.”

Check the White Mountain National Forest Facebook page and the Watch Duty app for updates on the status of the Wild River Fire.

Featured image: Gilead Maine Volunteer Fire Department

KLM Plans 1,000 More Job Cuts

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

KLM expects to eliminate approximately 1,000 positions through productivity improvements as part of a restructuring announced Thursday.

The Dutch carrier said it would seek to limit the impact through natural attrition, voluntary arrangements and transfers. Other changes include moving about 800 ground services positions to outside providers and transferring 2,000 positions to Air France-KLM, including 1,500 at customer contact center Cygnific.

“But KLM’s future is not guaranteed,” CEO Marjan Rintel said in a statement, citing global instability, high fuel prices, and pressure on the industry.

KLM is targeting a structural productivity increase of at least 8% by 2028, alongside restrained wage growth. It said an additional €1 billion in annual earnings improvement is needed to fund investments.

The changes also affect its European fleet. KLM will replace planned Airbus A320s with Embraer E195-E2s, giving regional subsidiary Cityhopper a larger role. The A321neo will remain, while Boeing 737s and Embraer E190s will be phased out.

The announcement came as KLM reported a third-quarter operating profit of €458 million, up from €352 million a year earlier. For the first nine months of 2026, operating profit reached €526 million on revenue of €10.9 billion.

Despite the improvement, KLM said profitability remains insufficient to support its investment needs. Higher fuel costs and Middle East flight cancellations weighed on its passenger business, while Transavia faced competition and operational challenges.

“We need to increase it to more than 8% to secure a financially healthy future for KLM,” Chief Financial Officer Bas Brouns said of the airline’s profit margin.

Google Search Isn’t Your Only Option. Try One of These Other Free Search Engines Now

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Our expert, award-winning staff selects the products we cover and rigorously researches and tests our top picks. If you buy through our links, we may earn a commission.

Tech > Services & Software > Online > Online Services

These free Google alternatives make it easy to break up with the tech giant.

On Tuesday, Nov. 3, Google announced that starting next year AI tools will be turned off by default in software used by K-12 students. Teachers and administrators will have to enable the features for students to use them in Google Search.

“Search and AI are deeply integrated,” Chris Phillips, vice president for education at Google, said, according to the New York Times. He also said if a school bans Google’s AI tools, students “will not have access to Search, and they’ll see a message from their school that indicates it’s not available.”

If you’re a parent or educator, you should know you don’t have to use Google Search to teach your children. There are plenty of other free search engines you can try now.

Alternative search engines offer advantages over Google, including greater privacy in some instances. The main drawback to some of these engines is that they may lack comprehensive results. That means you might have to look through more results on other search engines than on Google. I found it helpful to be more specific with my search queries when using these other engines.

It’s also important to note that using these search engines in Google Chrome may prevent you from getting their full benefits. Here are five free alternatives to Google Search.

Brave is a privacy-focused search engine that blocks trackers, and it uses Google as a fallback option. What that means is if your Brave search doesn’t return a lot of results, you can enable the search engine to anonymously check Google for the same query.

Brave also offers other features, like a VPN, a crypto wallet and its own AI assistant named Leo. And Brave said your AI usage is private and secure as well. You don’t even need to create or log into an account to use Leo.

DuckDuckGo is another privacy-focused search engine, and it has touted itself as the anti-Google. While Google collects and stores data on you to create personalized search results, DuckDuckGo doesn’t track what you do and actively blocks external trackers.

Over the past few years, DuckDuckGo also expanded the free tools it offers to people. Now it offers tools like an App Tracking Protection for Android users, its own VPN — that our experts said was great for general privacy and streaming — and an AI tool called DuckAssist.

This private, Berlin-based search engine is also a green option. Ecosia writes online it has planted over 250 million trees and produces more clean energy than it takes to power all its searches and AI queries. The company also writes online that all its profits go toward climate action.

This French-based search engine writes online it doesn’t retain your search data or sell your personal data, making it a good alternative to Google. In addition to search, Qwant also offers a feature called Qwant Junior, which it writes online is meant to help children between the ages of 6 and 12 years old safely explore the internet.

You should know that Qwant has integrated AI into its engine in what it calls Flash Answers. But you can deactivate it from your Settings menu without jumping through hoops. Historically, Qwant has used Bing’s search API, which lets companies access Bing’s search infrastructure. But according to CNBC, the company partnered with Berlin-based search engine Ecosia in 2024 to build a European search index in order to reduce dependence on US companies.

Startpage is a search engine that’s also a search aggregator. That means it partners with other companies like Google and Microsoft Bing to bring you search results.

For more on other search engines, here’s what to know about DuckDuckGo and Brave Search.
