Five9 Agrees to Buy Level AI and Broaden CX AI Distribution

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Five9 announced on November 5, 2026 that it had entered a definitive agreement to acquire Level AI, valuing the customer-experience AI company at approximately $325 million in cash. Five9 plans to pay from its balance sheet, and its board unanimously approved the agreement.

The transaction has not closed. Under the plan, Level AI’s applications would be sold both inside Five9’s Genius CX Platform and as standalone products for organizations using other contact-center systems.

Enterprises with on-premises contact centers, or those already using another cloud platform, could adopt Level AI without first buying Five9’s contact-center-as-a-service platform. The same applications would also join the AI application layer within Genius CX, giving existing Five9 customers an integrated route.

Chief Executive Amit Mathradas said the standalone option is intended for enterprises that want AI before they are ready to migrate their contact centers to the cloud. He also described the acquisition as a way to add experienced AI-native builders and more tools for research, analysis and planning, allowing human agents to concentrate on higher-value and complex customer interactions.

Level AI’s suite combines AI agents for voice and digital interactions, Agent Assist, quality management, Voice of the Customer, coaching and AI Workers on a shared conversation-intelligence layer. Its AI agents handle inquiries directly, Agent Assist guides human agents during live conversations, and AI Workers perform research and analysis previously handled manually by coaches and analysts.

Five9 expects the native applications to improve agent performance, automate more interactions across voice and digital channels, and convert customer conversations into insights usable across an organization. The platform runs on Level AI Latitude, a proprietary family of models built specifically for customer conversations.

Level AI reports that its platform handles more than one billion customer interactions annually for enterprises including Gusto, Chime, Empyrean and VistaPrint, and that Latitude delivers up to 49 times lower cost and four times lower latency than frontier models while giving enterprises direct control over data privacy.

The companies have partnered since 2022, with Level AI applications integrated with Five9 and available through the Five9 CX Marketplace. Five9 says the acquisition would make Level AI’s applications available to its base of more than 4,000 customers, adding an established distribution channel to the existing technical relationship.

Five9’s merger report filed with the SEC specifies that the consideration covers Level AI’s outstanding shares, options and warrants, subject to customary adjustments for cash, indebtedness, transaction expenses and net working capital at closing. Level AI would survive the merger as a wholly owned Five9 subsidiary.

Level AI stockholders holding sufficient voting power to approve the transaction signed support agreements. After the specified written consent is delivered, no further Level AI stockholder approval will be required. The merger agreement can be terminated if closing has not occurred by February 5, 2027, among other specified circumstances, and neither party is required to pay a termination fee.

Until closing, the companies will operate separately. Level AI says there are no immediate changes to customer contracts, invoices or support contacts and that it will provide advance notice of any change.

Five9’s third-quarter results, also dated November 5, 2026, provide the financial backdrop for the balance-sheet purchase. Revenue for the quarter ended September 30, 2026 rose 13% year over year to $323.3 million, with subscription revenue up 16%. At that date, Five9 reported $263.8 million in cash and cash equivalents and $433.8 million in marketable investments.

Five9 expects the acquisition to close in the fourth quarter of 2026, subject to customary closing conditions. Its fourth-quarter and full-year guidance excludes any contribution from the pending transaction, meaning Level AI is not yet reflected in the company’s published outlook.

The standalone route distinguishes the acquisition from a simple addition to Five9’s cloud suite: it creates a way to reach enterprises before they migrate, while the integrated route serves Five9’s existing base. Whether Five9 can extend Level AI’s reach while preserving that cross-platform continuity is the central operational question once the transaction closes.

Jen Affleck's Family Guide: Meet Estranged Husband, Kids Amid Divorce

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Jen Affleck‘s family life has been front and center ever since she joined the cast of The Secret Lives of Mormon Wives — and the Hulu star’s household is about to grow once again.

Us confirmed the reality TV personality filed for divorce from husband Zac Affleck in November 2026 in Utah, just months after announcing her pregnancy with the couple’s fourth baby. Jen and Zac initially met on Mormon dating app Mutual in 2018 and got married the following year.

Zac married Jen in 2019 at a local Church of Jesus Christ of Latter-day Saints temple after proposing at Top of the Rock in New York City. Their marriage played out publicly across multiple seasons of The Secret Lives of Mormon Wives, including a brief separation documented in season 2.

“We wouldn’t have been able to get through season 2 — honestly season 1 or season 2 — without therapy,” Jen told Us in May 2025. “Filming definitely isn’t a comfortable thing for Zac. I think it’s definitely harder for him to be vulnerable.”

The pair moved from Utah to the Los Angeles area in fall 2025 while Jen competed on season 34 of Dancing With the Stars. They stayed in California after her elimination.

Zac has since documented life as a stay-at-home dad on social media. One day before Jen’s divorce filing, he posted a TikTok from the beach with their youngest, captioning it, “Raising kids by the beach >> #sunset #ocean.”

Nora is the estranged couple’s eldest child. She was born in November 2021, when Jen and Zac were still in the early years of their marriage.

“Nora brings so much joy and light into our lives, and I feel incredibly blessed to be her mom 🥺,” Jen wrote via Instagram around Nora’s 3rd birthday. “There are countless things that I adore about her—she’s spunky, kind, and full of life. … To us, she truly is everything.”

Jen and Zac welcomed baby No. 2, son Luca, in July 2023.

“We had one and then a second baby back to back. Jen — like, six months after she gave birth — she got pregnant again with her second baby,” Zac said on “The Viall Files” in May 2025, claiming, “I got tricked into it. She took out her IUD without telling me.”

Jen laughed off the revelation and added, “Whoopsies, I forgot to tell you [I took my IUD out].”

Daughter Penelope — nicknamed Penny — was born in late June 2025, days after Jen and Zac’s wedding anniversary.

“She is perfect,” Jen wrote via Instagram in July 2025. “Going unmedicated was truly the most painful but empowering experience of my life. I could not have done it without Zac and my supportive doula! I’m so grateful and so happy that Penny is earth side now.”

In June 2026, Jen announced she was expecting baby No. 4, sharing a photo cradling her bump via Instagram with the caption, “Chapter Four. 🤍.”

Her pregnancy has not been easy, with Jen revealing the next month that she was experiencing prenatal depression for the second time.

“Prenatal depression came back this pregnancy. Only this time, it came back even heavier,” she wrote via Instagram. “There were many days during my first trimester where my mind went to some really dark places. It scared me.”

Jen also acknowledged the difficulty of making a cross-country move, noting, “Add moving to a new state, being pregnant, and trying to show up for your kids, and some days felt impossible,” she wrote, noting how friends, family and strangers helped carry her through the hardest moments.
